MTH1W Answer Keys and Explanations

Numbered to match the practice tests in your printed study guide.

Practice Test 1 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (-7 is less than -3): on a number line, values further to the left are smaller, and -7 sits farther left than -3. A common mistake is thinking -7 is bigger because 7 is a larger digit than 3, but the negative sign flips that idea completely. Option B has the comparison backwards, option C ignores that the two points are in different positions, and option D is false since -7 is actually farther from zero than -3.
  2. The correct answer is C (-1/3): using a common denominator of 12, -1/3 becomes -4/12 and -1/4 becomes -3/12, and -4/12 sits farther left on the number line. With negative fractions, the one with the bigger size ignoring the sign is actually the smaller value. Option A has the comparison backwards, option B is wrong since the fractions have different values, and option D is incorrect because fractions can be compared directly with a common denominator.
  3. The correct answer is C (-2, -1.5, 0.4, 2/3): converting everything to decimals gives -2, -1.5, 0.4, and about 0.67, which line up in that exact order from smallest to largest. Option A has the two negative numbers swapped, option B puts the two positive values in the wrong order, and option D lists the numbers from largest to smallest instead of smallest to largest.
  4. The correct answer is D (-8): when adding two numbers with the same sign, add their sizes and keep that sign, so 3 + 5 = 8 stays negative. Option C drops the negative sign entirely, and options A and B come from subtracting the two numbers instead of adding them.
  5. The correct answer is C (-3): when adding numbers with different signs, subtract the smaller size from the larger size and keep the sign of the number with the bigger size, so 7 - 4 = 3 stays negative since 7 was negative. Option B drops the negative sign, and options A and D come from adding the sizes together instead of subtracting.
  6. The correct answer is A (8): subtracting a negative number is the same as adding the opposite, so 5 - (-3) becomes 5 + 3, which equals 8. Options B and D come from forgetting to flip the subtraction sign and treating it as 5 - 3, and option C drops the correct positive sign.
  7. The correct answer is B (3): rewriting the problem as adding the opposite gives -6 + 9, and since the sizes are different, subtract 6 from 9 to get 3, keeping the positive sign since 9 was the bigger size. Option C has the correct size but the wrong sign, and options A and D come from adding the two sizes together instead of subtracting.
  8. The correct answer is A (-6): rewriting as adding the opposite gives 2 + (-8), and since the sizes are different, subtract 2 from 8 to get 6, keeping the negative sign since 8 was the bigger size. Option B has the right size but the wrong sign, and options D and C come from adding the two sizes together instead of subtracting.
  9. The correct answer is B (20): when multiplying two numbers with the same sign, the answer is positive, so a negative times a negative gives a positive result. Option A has the correct size but the wrong sign, and options C and D come from adding the numbers instead of multiplying them.
  10. The correct answer is D (-20): when multiplying two numbers with different signs, the answer is negative, so a negative times a positive gives a negative result. Option A has the correct size but the wrong sign, and options C and B come from subtracting the numbers instead of multiplying them.
  11. The correct answer is D (5): when dividing two numbers with the same sign, the answer is positive, so a negative divided by a negative gives a positive result. Option A has the correct size but the wrong sign, and options B and C come from adding or multiplying the numbers instead of dividing them.
  12. The correct answer is D (-5): when dividing two numbers with different signs, the answer is negative, so a positive divided by a negative gives a negative result. Option A has the correct size but the wrong sign, and options B and C come from adding or multiplying the numbers instead of dividing them.
  13. The correct answer is B (-24): count the negative signs in the expression, and since there are three (an odd number), the final answer is negative. Multiplying the sizes gives 2 × 3 × 1 × 4 = 24, so the answer is -24. Option A drops the negative sign, and options C and D come from adding the numbers together instead of multiplying them.
  14. The correct answer is A (30): count the negative signs, and since there are two (an even number), the final answer is positive. Multiplying the sizes gives 2 × 3 × 5 = 30. Option B drops the correct positive sign, and options C and D come from adding the numbers instead of multiplying them.
  15. The correct answer is C (1 1/6): using a common denominator of 6, 1/2 becomes 3/6 and 2/3 becomes 4/6, and adding the numerators gives 7/6, which is the same as 1 and 1/6. Option A comes from adding the numerators and denominators straight across without finding a common denominator, and options B and D are close but do not match the exact sum.
  16. The correct answer is A (-1 1/4): rewriting as adding the opposite gives -3/4 + (-1/2), and using a common denominator of 4, -1/2 becomes -2/4, so -3/4 + (-2/4) = -5/4, which is the same as -1 and 1/4. Option D comes from a subtraction error with the wrong common denominator, option B drops the negative sign, and option C adds an extra whole number by mistake.
  17. The correct answer is D (2/5): multiply the numerators to get 2 × 3 = 6 and multiply the denominators to get 3 × 5 = 15, giving 6/15, which simplifies to 2/5 by dividing top and bottom by 3. Option B comes from adding the numerators and denominators instead of multiplying, option C comes from cross multiplying incorrectly, and option A is the reciprocal of option C, another result of mixing up the multiplication steps.
  18. The correct answer is A (1 7/8): flip the second fraction to get its reciprocal, 5/2, then multiply, 3/4 × 5/2 = 15/8, which equals 1 and 7/8. Option B comes from multiplying without flipping the second fraction at all, option C comes from flipping the wrong fraction, and option D is not the result of any correct step in the process.
  19. The correct answer is A (3.45): adding a negative number is the same as subtracting, so 4.75 + (-1.3) becomes 4.75 - 1.3, which equals 3.45. Option C comes from treating the negative sign as positive and adding the two numbers together, option B has a sign error mixed into the subtraction, and option D comes from lining up the decimal points incorrectly.
  20. The correct answer is A (0.24): multiply as if there were no decimal points, 6 × 4 = 24, then count the total decimal places in both numbers, which is two, and place the decimal point two spots from the right. Option D places the decimal point in the wrong spot by moving it only one place, option B comes from adding the two numbers instead of multiplying, and option C moves the decimal point too many places.
  21. The correct answer is D (11): first solve the brackets, 5 - 1 = 4, then the exponent, 4² = 16, giving 3 + 2 × 16 divided by 4. Multiplication and division come next, left to right, 2 × 16 = 32 and 32 divided by 4 = 8, leaving 3 + 8 = 11. Option C skips the exponent step entirely, and options B and A come from applying the operations in the wrong order.
  22. The correct answer is A (0): inside the brackets, -3 + 7 = 4 and 2² = 4, so the bracket becomes 4 - 4 = 0, and any number multiplied by 0 is 0. Option B comes from treating the inner brackets as separate terms instead of one subtraction, option C comes from adding instead of subtracting inside the brackets, and option D ignores the outer multiplication by -2 altogether.
  23. The correct answer is B (-6): first solve the brackets using a common denominator of 4, 1/2 becomes 2/4, and 2/4 + 1/4 = 3/4, then multiply 3/4 × (-8) to get -24/4, which simplifies to -6. Option A comes from adding the fractions without a common denominator, option C comes from multiplying before adding the fractions in the brackets, and option D drops the negative sign.
  24. The correct answer is C (8): division and multiplication have equal priority and must be worked left to right as they appear, so 12 divided by 3 = 4 first, then 4 × 2 = 8. Option A comes from wrongly multiplying 3 × 2 first and then dividing 12 by that product, and options B and D come from combining the numbers in an order that BEDMAS does not allow.
  25. The correct answer is A (128): the product rule says that when multiplying powers with the same base, keep the base and add the exponents, so 2³ × 2⁴ = 2⁷, which equals 128. Option B comes from multiplying the exponents instead of adding them, option C comes from subtracting the exponents, and option D is only 2⁶, one power too small.
  26. The correct answer is C (216): the quotient rule says that when dividing powers with the same base, keep the base and subtract the exponents, so 6⁵ divided by 6² = 6³, which equals 216. Option A comes from adding the exponents instead of subtracting, option B comes from subtracting too far down to 6¹, and option D is 6⁶, one power too many.
  27. The correct answer is A (6561): the power of a power rule says to keep the base and multiply the exponents, so (3²)⁴ = 3⁸, which equals 6561. Option C comes from adding the exponents instead of multiplying, option B comes from forgetting the outer exponent and only calculating 3⁴, and option D only calculates 3².
  28. The correct answer is B (1): any nonzero number raised to the exponent zero equals 1, which is the zero exponent rule. Option A confuses the exponent with the base itself, option D treats the exponent as if it did nothing at all, and option C has no mathematical basis under this rule.
  29. The correct answer is A (1/8): a negative exponent means to take the reciprocal, so 2⁻³ means 1/(2³), which equals 1/8. Option B treats the negative sign as if it belonged to the base instead of the exponent, and options C and D both drop the reciprocal step needed for a negative exponent.
  30. The correct answer is C (-8): (-2)³ means (-2) × (-2) × (-2), and with three negative signs (an odd number), the result is negative, giving -8. Option A drops the negative sign, and options B and D come from multiplying 2 × 3 instead of multiplying -2 by itself three times.
  31. The correct answer is C (-9): without brackets around the -3, the exponent only applies to the 3, so -3² really means -(3²) = -9. Option A confuses this with (-3)², which does equal 9 because the brackets would include the negative sign, and options B and D come from multiplying 3 × 2 instead of squaring.
  32. The correct answer is C (72): since the bases 2 and 3 are different, the product rule does not apply, so each power must be calculated separately, 2³ = 8 and 3² = 9, and 8 × 9 = 72. Option A wrongly combines the two into 6⁵ by adding the bases and keeping both exponents, and options B and D come from other incorrect ways of combining the bases and exponents.
  33. The correct answer is B (5): rounding 6.8 to 7 and 3.2 to 3 gives 7 + 3 = 10, then 10 divided by 2 = 5, which matches the exact answer closely. Option A forgets to divide by 2 at the end, option C only rounds one of the two numbers, and option D comes from rounding both numbers down too far.
  34. The correct answer is D (10): 79 is close to the compatible number 80, and 80 divided by 8 = 10, which is a fast and reasonably close estimate since the exact answer is 9.875. Option A mistakes the divisor for the estimated quotient, and options C and B are not the result of rounding to a nearby compatible number.
  35. The correct answer is A (1): 7/8 is close to 1 and 1/9 is close to 0, so the sum should land near 1, which matches the exact answer of 71/72. Option C wrongly treats both fractions as close to 1/2, option B overestimates by rounding both fractions up to 1, and option D comes from adding the numerators and denominators straight across.
  36. The correct answer is B (-13): the afternoon temperature is -12 + 8 = -4 degrees, and dropping 9 more degrees overnight gives -4 + (-9) = -13 degrees. Option A only shows the afternoon temperature and stops before the overnight drop, option D comes from adding the sizes of both changes together instead of combining signs correctly, and option C comes from a sign error in the final step.
  37. The correct answer is A (-110 metres): starting at -150 metres and rising 40 metres gives -150 + 40 = -110 metres, meaning the submarine is still 110 metres below sea level. Option B comes from subtracting 40 instead of adding it, option C drops the negative sign entirely, and option D comes from an arithmetic error in combining the two values.
  38. The correct answer is A (-6.25 dollars): the deposit gives -45.50 + 60 = 14.50 dollars, and spending 20.75 dollars gives 14.50 - 20.75 = -6.25 dollars. Option B comes from adding the deposit and the spending together instead of subtracting the spending, option C comes from subtracting the deposit instead of adding it, and option D stops after only the first step.
  39. The correct answer is A (1 1/8 cups): since 1.5 equals 3/2, the calculation is 3/4 × 3/2 = 9/8, which equals 1 and 1/8 cups. Option C comes from a fraction multiplication error, option B comes from tripling the recipe instead of using 1.5 times, and option D comes from dividing instead of multiplying.
  40. The correct answer is D (-8): using a common denominator of 6, -2/3 becomes -4/6, so -4/6 + 1/6 = -3/6, which simplifies to -1/2, and (-4)² = 16, so -1/2 × 16 = -8. Option A drops the negative sign from the fraction step, option C comes from forgetting to square the -4, and option B makes both the squaring error and a sign error.
  41. The correct answer is A (2): using the product rule first, 2³ × 2² = 2⁵, and then the quotient rule, 2⁵ divided by 2⁴ = 2¹, which equals 2. Option B comes from adding all three exponents to get 2⁹ instead of subtracting in the division step, option C comes from subtracting the exponents down too far, and option D comes from forgetting to divide by 2⁴ at all.
  42. The correct answer is C (-7 metres): the total drop over 4 hours is 4 × (-3) = -12 metres, and adding this to the starting elevation gives 5 + (-12) = -7 metres. Option A only calculates the total drop and forgets to add the starting elevation, option B drops the negative sign from the final answer, and option D comes from multiplying the wrong pair of numbers together.
  43. The correct answer is C (-5/6 is less than -7/9): using a common denominator of 18, -5/6 becomes -15/18 and -7/9 becomes -14/18, and since -15/18 sits farther left on the number line, -5/6 is the smaller value. Option A has the comparison backwards, option B is wrong since the two fractions convert to different values, and option D contains a false claim since -7/9 cannot be greater than 0.
  44. The correct answer is C (-1/2 is greater than -3/4): -1/2 equals -0.5 and -3/4 equals -0.75, and since -0.5 sits farther right on the number line, -1/2 is the larger value. Option A has the comparison backwards, option B ignores that the two points sit at different positions, and option D is false since -1/2 is actually closer to zero than -3/4.
  45. The correct answer is B (198 metres): the total descent is 4 × 15 = 60 metres, and 2³ = 8 metres, so the calculation is 250 - 60 + 8 = 198 metres. Option A comes from subtracting the 2³ = 8 metre rise instead of adding it, option C forgets both the descent and the rise, and option D comes from descending for 8 minutes instead of 4, giving 250 - 15 × 8 = 130.
  46. The correct answer is B (√2): √2 equals approximately 1.41421356... and never repeats a pattern or terminates, which makes it irrational. 0.6666... is rational because it repeats forever in a pattern equal to 2/3. 5/9 is rational because it is already written as a fraction of two integers. -3 is rational because it can be written as -3/1.
  47. The correct answer is D (-7/2, -3.2, -3, 1/2): converting -7/2 to a decimal gives -3.5, which is the smallest, followed by -3.2, then -3, then 1/2 as the largest. Option A puts -3 before the more negative numbers, which is backwards. Option C lists the numbers from greatest to least instead of least to greatest. Option B swaps the order of -3.2 and -3.5, ignoring which one sits farther left on the number line.
  48. The correct answer is C (Q): point Q is at 3, which has an absolute value of 3, while point P is at -4, which has an absolute value of 4. Since 3 is smaller than 4, point Q is closer to zero. Option A is wrong because P is actually farther from zero. Option B is wrong because the two points are not the same distance from zero, and option D is wrong because the distance can be found directly from the number line.
  49. The correct answer is B (-23): both numbers are negative, so add their sizes, 8 + 15 = 23, and keep the negative sign. Option A comes from subtracting instead of adding the sizes. Options D and C drop the negative sign, giving a positive result when both original numbers were negative.
  50. The correct answer is A (-7): the numbers have different signs, so subtract the smaller size from the larger, 19 - 12 = 7, and keep the sign of the larger size, which is negative. Option B drops the negative sign. Options C and D come from adding the sizes together instead of subtracting them.
  51. The correct answer is D (-23): subtracting 9 from -14 means moving 9 more units to the left on the number line, so -14 - 9 = -23. Options A and C come from treating this like -14 + 9 instead of -14 - 9. Option B drops the negative sign entirely.
  52. The correct answer is B (17): subtracting a negative number is the same as adding the opposite, so 6 - (-11) becomes 6 + 11 = 17. Options A and C forget to flip the subtraction into addition and instead subtract 11 from 6. Option D keeps the correct size but gives it the wrong sign.
  53. The correct answer is A (4): rewrite the expression as adding the opposite, -10 + 14. The numbers have different signs, so subtract 10 from 14 to get 4, and since 14 was the larger size and positive, the answer is positive 4. Options D and B add the sizes together instead of subtracting them, and option C gives the correct size but the wrong sign.
  54. The correct answer is B (-107 metres): start at -85, add 30 for the rise to get -55, then subtract 52 for the descent to get -107. Option A comes from subtracting both changes instead of adding the rise first. Option C comes from adding both changes as if the submarine rose twice, calculating -85 + 30 + 52 instead of subtracting the descent. Option D drops the negative sign, showing the submarine above sea level instead of below.
  55. The correct answer is D (42): both numbers are negative, which is the same sign, so the product is positive, and 7 × 6 = 42. Option A keeps the correct size but gives it the wrong sign. Options C and B come from adding 7 and 6 instead of multiplying them.
  56. The correct answer is C (-36): the numbers have different signs, so the product is negative, and 9 × 4 = 36. Option A keeps the correct size but makes it positive. Options B and D come from adding 9 and 4 instead of multiplying.
  57. The correct answer is B (9): both numbers are negative, which is the same sign, so the quotient is positive, and 72 divided by 8 is 9. Option A keeps the correct size but gives it the wrong sign. Options D and C come from multiplying 8 by itself instead of dividing 72 by 8.
  58. The correct answer is D (-8): the numbers have different signs, so the quotient is negative, and 56 divided by 7 is 8. Option B keeps the correct size but makes it positive. Options C and A come from subtracting 7 from 56 instead of dividing.
  59. The correct answer is B (30): there are four negative numbers being multiplied, which is an even count, so the final answer is positive, and 3 × 2 × 1 × 5 = 30. Option A wrongly treats the count of negatives as odd. Options C and D come from adding the numbers' sizes instead of multiplying them.
  60. The correct answer is D (-60): there are three negative numbers, which is an odd count, so the final answer is negative, and multiplying the sizes gives 4 × 5 × 3 × 1 = 60. Option A drops the negative sign. Options B and C come from adding some of the numbers together instead of multiplying all four.
  61. The correct answer is D (1/2): using a common denominator of 6, 1/3 becomes 2/6, and 2/6 + 1/6 = 3/6, which simplifies to 1/2. Option A comes from adding the numerators and denominators straight across (1+1 over 3+6). Option B only counts the second fraction, and option C comes from adding 2/3 and 1/6 by mistake instead of the given fractions.
  62. The correct answer is A (-11/12): using a common denominator of 12, -2/3 becomes -8/12 and 1/4 becomes 3/12, and rewriting the subtraction as adding the opposite gives -8/12 + (-3/12) = -11/12. Option B comes from subtracting the numerators incorrectly. Option C keeps the correct size but drops the negative sign, and option D comes from subtracting only the second fraction's original denominator without converting.
  63. The correct answer is C (1/2): multiply the numerators, 4 × 5 = 20, and the denominators, 5 × 8 = 40, giving 20/40, which simplifies to 1/2. Option A comes from adding the fractions instead of multiplying them. Options B and D come from simplifying incorrectly or multiplying the wrong pairs of numbers.
  64. The correct answer is B (5/4): flip the second fraction to get its reciprocal, 3/2, then multiply, 5/6 × 3/2 = 15/12, which simplifies to 5/4. Option A comes from dividing the numerators and denominators incorrectly. Option C comes from multiplying without flipping either fraction, and option D flips the first fraction instead of the second, giving the reciprocal of the correct answer.
  65. The correct answer is D (-3.25): the numbers have different signs, so subtract the smaller size from the larger, 5.6 - 2.35 = 3.25, and keep the sign of the larger size, which is negative. Option A drops the negative sign. Options B and C come from adding the sizes together instead of subtracting them.
  66. The correct answer is D (0.056): multiply as if there were no decimal points, 7 × 8 = 56, then count the total decimal places in both numbers, which is three (one from 0.7 and two from 0.08), and place the point three spots from the right. Option B places the decimal point only two spots from the right. Options C and A place the decimal point in the wrong direction entirely.
  67. The correct answer is D (4.95 dollars): start at -32.40, add the 50 dollar deposit to get 17.60, then subtract the 12.65 dollar purchase to get 4.95. Option A gives the correct size but the wrong sign. Option C comes from adding the deposit and the purchase together instead of subtracting the purchase, and option B comes from a small subtraction error.
  68. The correct answer is B (0.454545...): this decimal repeats the pattern 45 forever, which makes it a rational number equal to 5/11. The other three decimals, pi, the square root of 3, and Euler's number, all continue forever without ever repeating a pattern, which makes them irrational.
  69. The correct answer is C (8): first solve the brackets, 7 - 4 = 3, then the exponent, 3² = 9, then multiply and divide left to right, 3 × 9 = 27 and 27 / 9 = 3, and finally add, 5 + 3 = 8. Option A comes from working left to right and ignoring the order of operations completely. Option B forgets the division step. Option D applies the exponent to the 4 before solving the brackets.
  70. The correct answer is D (-9): solve inside the brackets first, 4 - 9 = -5 and 2³ = 8, so -5 + 8 = 3, then multiply, -3 × 3 = -9. Option B drops the negative sign from the multiplication. Option C comes from calculating 2³ as 2 × 3 = 6 instead of 2 × 2 × 2 = 8. Option A comes from ignoring the brackets and working left to right instead.
  71. The correct answer is D (-3): solve the brackets first, 3/4 - 1/2 = 1/4 using a common denominator of 4, then multiply, 1/4 × (-12) = -3. Option A drops the negative sign. Option C comes from miscalculating the bracket as 1/2 instead of 1/4. Option B comes from multiplying only the 3/4 term by -12 and ignoring the 1/2 term.
  72. The correct answer is D (9): solve the brackets, 6 - 2 = 4, then the exponent, 2³ = 8, then multiply and divide left to right, 4 × 4 = 16 and 16 / 8 = 2, and finally add and subtract left to right, 8 + 2 - 1 = 9. Option A comes from adding before multiplying, breaking the correct order. Option B comes from calculating 2³ as 6 instead of 8. Option C forgets to subtract the final 1.
  73. The correct answer is C (4⁷): when multiplying powers with the same base, keep the base and add the exponents, 2 + 5 = 7. Option A comes from multiplying the exponents instead of adding them. Option B mistakenly multiplies the bases together as well. Option D comes from subtracting the exponents instead of adding them.
  74. The correct answer is A (9⁵): when dividing powers with the same base, keep the base and subtract the exponents, 8 - 3 = 5. Option B comes from adding the exponents instead of subtracting them. Option C comes from multiplying the exponents. Option D mistakenly squares the base as well as changing the exponent.
  75. The correct answer is B (5⁶): when raising a power to another power, keep the base and multiply the exponents, 3 × 2 = 6. Option A comes from adding the exponents instead of multiplying them. Option C comes from raising the exponent 3² instead of multiplying it by 2. Option D mistakenly squares the base as well.
  76. The correct answer is B (1): any nonzero number raised to the exponent zero equals 1, no matter what the base is. Option A wrongly attaches the negative sign from the base. Option C confuses the zero exponent rule with multiplying by zero. Option D mistakenly returns the base itself instead of applying the rule.
  77. The correct answer is A (1/81): a negative exponent means take the reciprocal, so 3⁻⁴ becomes 1/(3⁴), and 3⁴ = 81, giving 1/81. Option D treats the negative sign as if it belonged to the answer instead of the exponent. Option B miscalculates 3⁴ as 12. Option C forgets to take the reciprocal at all.
  78. The correct answer is D (1/36): a negative exponent means take the reciprocal of the base raised to the positive exponent, so 6⁻² becomes 1/(6²), and 6² = 36, giving 1/36. Option B forgets to take the reciprocal. Option C miscalculates 6² as 12 instead of 36. Option A keeps the correct fraction but adds an unnecessary negative sign.
  79. The correct answer is C (-5² equals -25 and (-5)² equals 25): without brackets, the exponent applies only to the 5, so -5² means -(5²) = -25, but with brackets, (-5)² means (-5) × (-5) = 25. Option A wrongly assumes the negative sign is always included in the base. Option B swaps the two results. Option D wrongly assumes the negative sign always stays outside the exponent's effect.
  80. The correct answer is D (72): the product rule only works when the bases are the same, but here the bases are 3 and 2, so each power must be calculated separately, 3² = 9 and 2³ = 8, and 9 × 8 = 72. Option A calculates the student's incorrect claim, 6⁵, which equals 7776 but is not the real value. Option B comes from adding 9 and 8 instead of multiplying them. Option C comes from miscalculating 2³ as 6 instead of 8 before multiplying.
  81. The correct answer is D (15): rounding 39.7 to 40 and 20.4 to 20 gives 40 + 20 = 60, and 60 divided by 4 = 15, which matches the exact answer of about 15.03. Option A comes from dividing by too large a number. Option B comes from dividing the rounded sum of 60 by 3 instead of 4. Option C rounds both numbers down too aggressively.
  82. The correct answer is A (20): using the compatible number 120 instead of 123, since 120 divides evenly by 6, gives 120 / 6 = 20, which is very close to the exact answer of 20.5.   83. Option A rounds both fractions up to a full 1 each. Option B only counts one of the two fractions. Option D uses only the smaller fraction's benchmark value.

  85. The correct answer is D (-28 metres): starting at -22, subtract 15 for the further descent to get -37, then add 9 for the ascent to get -28.   86. The correct answer is C (19.75 dollars): starting at -18.75, add the 45 dollar deposit to get 26.25, then subtract the 6.50 dollar fee to get 19.75.   87. The correct answer is C (1 2/3 cups): rewrite 2.5 as the fraction 5/2, then multiply, 2/3 × 5/2 = 10/6, which simplifies to 5/3, or 1 and 2/3 cups. Option A comes from a simplification error.   88. The correct answer is C (-3 ≤ x < 5): the filled-in circle at -3 means -3 itself is included, shown with a less than or equal to symbol, while the open circle at 5 means 5 itself is not included, shown with a plain less than symbol. Option A includes 5 but not -3, which is backwards. Option B excludes both endpoints. Option D wrongly includes both endpoints.
  89. The correct answer is B (6 units): the distance between two points on a number line is the absolute value of their difference, 4 - (-2) = 6. Option A comes from subtracting the sizes instead of finding the true gap between the points. Option D gives the correct size but a negative value, which distance can never be. Option C comes from miscounting the units between the two points and overshooting the true gap.
  90. The correct answer is A (-3): a number is greater than -5 if it sits farther to the right on the number line, and -3 is to the right of -5. Options B, C, and D are all farther to the left than -5, which makes them smaller, not greater, even though some of their digits look bigger.
  91. The correct answer is A (3:4): the greatest common factor of 15 and 20 is 5, so dividing both numbers by 5 gives 3:4. Option B reverses the order to girls to boys instead of boys to girls. Option C is the correct comparison but has not been simplified. Option D uses the wrong reduction and does not represent the same relationship as 15:20.
  92. The correct answer is B (Per hundred): percent literally means per hundred, so a percentage always compares a number to 100. Option A confuses percent with the prefix used for deci. Option C confuses percent with per mille, a different unit entirely. Option D describes a unit rate, not a percentage.

Practice Test 2 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (x² + 11x + 24): multiplying every term in the first bracket by every term in the second gives x², 8x, 3x, and 24. Collecting the middle terms, 8x plus 3x gives 11x, so the result is x² + 11x + 24. Option D forgets the two middle terms completely. Option B swaps the roles of the sum and the product of 3 and 8.
  2. The correct answer is D (x² - 3x - 10): the first terms give x², the outer terms give 2x, the inner terms give -5x, and the last terms give -10.   3. The correct answer is C (2x² - 9x - 5): multiplying out gives 2x², -10x, x, and -5, and collecting -10x and x gives -9x, so the answer is 2x² - 9x - 5. Option D makes an error combining the middle terms, treating -10x and x as if both were negative and adding to -11x.
  4. The correct answer is D (3x² + 10x - 8): multiplying out gives 3x², 12x, -2x, and -8, and collecting 12x and -2x gives 10x, so the full answer is 3x² + 10x - 8. Option A drops the coefficient on the first term, forgetting that 3x times x gives 3x², not just x².
  5. Option B flips the sign of the middle term to positive instead of keeping it negative.

Option A multiplies the exponents instead of adding them. Option B subtracts the exponents, which is the rule for dividing, not multiplying.

Option B divides the exponents instead of subtracting them. Option A multiplies the exponents, and option D adds them, which is the rule for multiplying powers, not dividing.

Favorable outcomes divided by total possible outcomes before any trials describes theoretical probability, not experimental. The largest value minus the smallest value describes range, an unrelated concept. Summing all outcomes and dividing by the number of trials is not the correct formula for experimental probability.
  245. The correct answer is A (0.6): dividing the 18 times heads occurred by the 30 total flips gives 18/30, which equals 0.6. 0.5 would be the theoretical probability of flipping heads on a fair coin, not the experimental result recorded here. 0.3 would come from dividing incorrectly or mixing up the numbers. 0.18 mistakenly uses 18 as a decimal instead of dividing it by the total number of trials.
  246. The correct answer is B (Experimental results come from real trials and often differ slightly from theoretical probability, especially with fewer trials): small differences between experimental and theoretical probability are normal and expected, and they tend to shrink as more trials are performed. A small mismatch does not mean the die is broken, this is a completely normal outcome. Theoretical probability is not 'always wrong', it is a prediction based on logic that experimental results tend to approach over many trials. There is no reason to assume a counting error just because the two values are close but not identical.
  247. The correct answer is D (A vertical axis that does not start at zero): a truncated axis cuts off the lower part of the scale, which can make small real differences between bars look much bigger than they actually are. An axis with unevenly skipping numbers describes an inconsistent scale, a related but different trick. An axis drawn in 3D describes the 3D visual exaggeration trick, not truncation. A truncated axis is about where the scale starts, not about whether the data is categorical or numerical.
  248. The correct answer is A (It makes the small real difference between 82 and 88 units look much larger than it actually is): since the axis starts at 80 instead of 0, the missing space is cut out of the picture, making a real difference of only 6 units look dramatic. The bars would not look the same height, since the truncated scale actually exaggerates their difference rather than hiding it. Bar graphs absolutely can be misleading, which is exactly the point of this warning. Product B did not sell about twice as much as Product A, that is a huge overstatement of a 6-unit difference out of over 80 units.
  249. The correct answer is C (Cherry-picking data): the company is only showing the short time period that supports their point while leaving out the longer trend that tells a more complete, less favorable story. A truncated axis is a graph-drawing trick about where the vertical scale starts, not about which time period is chosen to highlight. An inconsistent scale is about uneven spacing between numbers on an axis, a different problem. A random sample is actually a fair, unbiased way to collect data, the opposite of what is happening here.
  250. The correct answer is B (The eye focuses on the exaggerated visual size instead of the true percentage the slice represents): 3D effects distort how big a slice looks, causing viewers to misjudge the real percentage based on visual size rather than the actual number. The percentages can still be calculated correctly behind the scenes, the 3D effect only distorts how they appear visually. A 3D effect does not change the underlying data type from categorical to numerical. A misleading visual style has nothing to do with fixing sampling bias, those are two completely separate issues.

Practice Test 3 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (Categorical data): vehicle type describes a quality or category, not a number, so it cannot be averaged or measured on a number line. Discrete and continuous data are both numerical, but vehicle type has no numeric value at all. Experimental data refers to results from performing a trial, not the kind of variable being measured. Only counting how often each category appears makes sense here, which is the definition of categorical data.
  2. The correct answer is B (The number of goals scored in a hockey game): goals scored can only be whole numbers like 0, 1, 2, or 3, so it is discrete numerical data. Weight, height, and temperature can all take any decimal value within a range, such as 3.7 kg or 21.4 degrees, which makes them continuous instead. Discrete data comes from counting separate items, while continuous data comes from measuring. Since goals are counted one at a time with no fractions, they fit the discrete category.
  3. The correct answer is C (10): students owning 2 or more pets includes the 7 students who own exactly 2 and the 3 students who own exactly 3, so 7 + 3 = 10. Option A (7) only counts students with exactly 2 pets, and option D (3) only counts students with exactly 3 pets, both ignoring the other group. Option B (9) is the frequency for students who own exactly 1 pet, which does not meet the 'or more' condition. Adding the two highest frequency rows together gives the correct total of 10 students.
  4. The correct answer is D (The sample is biased and does not represent the whole school): only asking football players leaves out every other student, so the results are skewed toward one group's tastes. The sample size being too large is not the issue here, since the sample is actually too narrow, not too big. Favorite food is categorical data no matter who is asked, so option B is incorrect. This is a sample, not a census, because it does not include every member of the population, ruling out option C.
  5. The correct answer is C (22): each full bundle represents 5 tally marks, so 4 bundles equal 4 times 5, which is 20, plus the 2 extra marks gives 20 + 2 = 22. Option B (20) forgets to add the 2 extra marks left over after the bundles. Option A (18) incorrectly subtracts instead of adding the extra marks. Option D (24) mistakenly adds 4 extra marks instead of the 2 that were actually shown, so it overcounts the total.
  6. The correct answer is A (Histogram bars touch because the data is continuous, but bar graph bars have gaps because the categories are separate): histograms group continuous numerical data into ranges, so the bars are drawn touching to show the data flows smoothly from one interval to the next. Bar graphs represent categorical or discrete data, where each bar stands alone, so gaps are used between bars. Option B is false since both graphs use rectangular bars, not circles. Option C and D describe made-up limitations that have nothing to do with how these two graphs actually work.
  7. The correct answer is B (30%): dividing the favorable count by the total, 15 divided by 50 equals 0.3, and multiplying by 100 gives 30%. Option A (15%) mistakes the raw count of students for a percent instead of dividing it by the total. Option C (50%) is simply the total number of students surveyed, not a percent at all. Option D (35%) does not match the actual division and is not supported by the numbers given in the question.
  8. The correct answer is B (72): to convert a percent into a slice angle, multiply the percent as a decimal by 360 degrees, so 0.2 times 360 equals 72 degrees. Option A (36) would be the result of using 10% instead of 20%. Option D (90) matches a category worth 25%, not 20%. Option C (180) is the angle for a category that makes up 50% of the whole, which is far larger than 20%.
  9. The correct answer is C (Mean): the mean is found by adding every value in the data set together and then dividing that sum by how many values there are. The median instead requires sorting the values and picking the middle one, which is a different process entirely. The mode simply looks for the value that repeats most often, with no addition or division involved. Range is a measure of spread, found by subtracting the smallest value from the largest, not a measure of central tendency at all.
  10. The correct answer is C (1.1): multiply each sibling value by its frequency and add the results, giving (0 times 3) + (1 times 4) + (2 times 2) + (3 times 1) = 0 + 4 + 4 + 3 = 11, then divide by the total of 10 students to get 1.1. Option A (1) rounds incorrectly and does not match the actual calculation. Option B (2) confuses the highest sibling value with the mean. Option D (0.9) comes from dividing by the wrong total instead of the correct 10 students.
  11. The correct answer is C (The 5th value): with an odd number of values, the median is the single value sitting exactly in the middle, and for 9 values that is the 5th one, since there are 4 values below it and 4 above it. Option A and option B are the first and last values in the ordered list, which represent the minimum and maximum, not the median. Option D describes how to find the median only when there is an even number of values, which does not apply here since 9 is odd.
  12. The correct answer is A (6 and 9): both 6 and 9 appear exactly twice in the data set, while 4, 8, and 10 each appear only once, so the data set has two modes, making it bimodal. Option D (6 only) and option B (9 only) each miss one of the two values that are tied for the highest frequency. Option C (8) appears only once in the list, so it cannot be the mode at all. A data set can have more than one mode whenever two or more values are tied for the highest frequency, as is the case here.
  13. The correct answer is B (76): adding all five scores gives 68 + 72 + 75 + 81 + 84 = 380, and dividing by 5 gives 380 divided by 5, which equals 76. Option A (75) is simply the third score in the list, not the calculated mean. Option C (81) is the fourth score on its own, mistaken for the average of all five. Option D (72) is the second score in the list and does not account for the other four values.
  14. The correct answer is A (19): since there are 6 values, an even number, the median is the mean of the two middle values, which are 18 and 20, so (18 + 20) divided by 2 equals 19. Option B (18) and option C (20) are each only one of the two middle values on their own, not their average. Option D (21) does not match any correct calculation from this data set. Whenever a data set has an even count, always average the two middle numbers instead of picking just one.
  15.   16. The correct answer is C (19): the range is the largest value minus the smallest value, so 22 - 3 = 19. The range measures how spread out the data is. Option A is just the largest value on its own. Option B is the mean of the data set, and option D is the median, which are both measures of centre, not spread.
  17. The correct answer is A (9): the highest temperature in the table is 24 degrees on Thursday and the lowest is 15 degrees on Wednesday, so the range is 24 minus 15, which equals 9.   18. The correct answer is D (Positive correlation): when both variables increase together, causing the points to trend upward from left to right, that pattern is called a positive correlation. Negative correlation is the opposite pattern, where one variable increases while the other decreases, trending downward. No correlation means the points are scattered with no clear pattern at all, which does not match this description. Causation is a separate idea about one variable directly causing a change in another, which a scatter plot alone cannot prove.
  19. Option C is incorrect because a scatter plot can show a relationship but cannot prove that TV watching directly causes the lower scores.
  20. Option A and option B both wrongly assume a direct cause and effect link between two things that are only connected through a third factor, the weather. Option C is wrong because there clearly is a relationship, a correlation, even though it is not a causal one. This is the classic warning that a strong correlation between two variables does not prove that one directly causes the other.
